# Mutual Recognition (Equivalence of Gaming and Other Occupations) Declaration 2009

as amended

made under section 32 of the *Mutual Recognition Act 1992*

**Compilation start date:** 17 November 2015

**Includes amendments up to:** *Mutual Recognition (Equivalence of Gaming and Other Occupations) Amendment Declaration 2015 (F2015L01802)*

**About this compilation**

**This compilation**

This is a compilation of the *Mutual Recognition (Equivalence of Gaming and Other Occupations) Declaration 2009* as in force on 17 November 2015. It includes any commenced amendment affecting the legislation to that date.

This compilation was prepared on 15 December 2015.

The notes at the end of this compilation (the ***endnotes***) include information about amending laws and the amendment history of each amended provision.

**Uncommenced amendments**

The effect of uncommenced amendments is not reflected in the text of the compiled law but the text of the amendments is included in the endnotes.

**Application, saving and transitional provisions for provisions and amendments**

If the operation of a provision or amendment is affected by an application, saving or transitional provision that is not included in this compilation, details are included in the endnotes.

**Modifications**

If a provision of the compiled law is affected by a modification that is in force, details are included in the endnotes.

**Provisions ceasing to have effect**

If a provision of the compiled law has expired or otherwise ceased to have effect in accordance with a provision of the law, details are included in the endnotes.

## 1. Name of declaration

This Declaration is the Mutual Recognition (Equivalence of Gaming and Other Occupations) Declaration 2009.

## 2. Commencement

This Declaration commences on the day after it is registered.

## 3. Interpretation

(1) In this Declaration:

***Registration*** includes a licence, approval, admission, certificate or any other form of authorisation.

***Schedule*** means a schedule to this Declaration.

**Part 1 Casino and Gaming Occupations**

## 4. Equivalent casino occupations

(1) Subject to subsection (2), the occupation which may be carried on only by a person granted a registration, in the jurisdiction, described in Column A of Schedule 1 (***first jurisdiction***) is equivalent to the occupation which may be carried on only by a person granted a registration, in a jurisdiction, described in the same row of Schedule 1 (***second jurisdiction***), subject to any conditions, limitations or restrictions indicated in Schedule 1.

(2) The occupation which may be carried on only by a person granted a registration, in the jurisdiction, described in Rows 2, 4, 7, 8, 10, 11, 13, 16, 19, 22 and 23 of Column A of Schedule 1 (***first jurisdiction***) is, subject to any conditions, limitations or restrictions indicated in Schedule 1, equivalent to the occupation which may be carried on only by a person granted a registration, in the jurisdiction, described in the same row of Schedule 1 (***second jurisdiction***), where the registrations issued by both jurisdictions authorise the same activity, or same combination of listed activities, below:

(a) casino management;

(b) casino executive management;

(c) the conduct of gaming;

(d) gaming machine;

(e) keno gaming;

(f) the maintenance, construction, or repair of gaming equipment;

(g) the maintenance or repair of security and surveillance equipment;

(h) the maintenance and support of computer systems in the casino;

(i) the movement of money or chips about the casino (other than in relation to the supply of food and beverages);

(j) the storage and exchange of money or chips to patrons in the casino (other than in relation to the supply of food and beverages);

(k) the counting of money or chips in the casino (other than in relation to the supply of food and beverages);

(l) cash and accounting (other than in relation to the supply of food and beverages);

(m) internal audit operations;

(n) the storage of gaming equipment in the casino;

(o) casino promotions (if this is PR);

(p) security operations;

(q) surveillance operations;

(r) administrative and incidental operations.

(3) For the purposes of subsection (2), where a registration issued by the first jurisdiction does not identify the activities authorised by the registration, the registration is deemed to authorise only the activities in fact carried out by the registration holder in the first jurisdiction.

## 5. Declaration not to limit powers of a registration authority

(1) Nothing in this Part is intended to affect the powers, under the *Mutual Recognition Act 1992*, of a registration authority in a second jurisdiction to impose conditions on a registration, other than conditions that relate to the activities authorised to be carried out under the registration.

**Part 2 Shotfirers and Pyrotechnicians, Pest and Weed Controllers, Building Occupations, Builders (2006 Update)**

## 6. Equivalent occupations Shotfirers and Pyrotechnicians, Pest and Weed Controllers, Building Occupations, Builders (2006 Update)

(1) The occupation which may be carried on only by a person granted a registration, in the jurisdiction, described in Column A of the Schedule (***first jurisdiction***) is equivalent to the occupation which may be carried on only by a person granted a registration, in a jurisdiction, described in the same row of the Schedule (***second jurisdiction***), subject to any conditions, limitations or restrictions indicated in the Schedule.

## 7. Declaration not to limit powers of a registration authority

(1) Nothing in this Part is intended to affect the powers, under the *Mutual Recognition Act 1992*, of a registration authority in a second jurisdiction to impose conditions on a registration, provided that the conditions imposed do not relate to the activities authorised to be carried out under the registration unless such conditions applied to the person's registration in the first jurisdiction.

**Notes:**

The table at the attached Schedule depicts the registration outcome for any person already licensed in an occupation in a particular jurisdiction (column A of each row) should they seek to be licensed in an occupation in a second jurisdiction (potentially in any other column in the same row). Registration shall be granted by the second jurisdiction in the terms specified provided the applicant is already registered in the equivalent occupation in the first jurisdiction.

If a licence is not included in the declaration, or if the licence outcome depicted on the Schedule is the statement ‘No equivalent declared’, the decision on licence recognition will be made by the relevant registration authority in accordance with the other provisions of the *Mutual Recognition Act 1992*.

Licences covered by this declaration are licences that are issued to natural persons, not licences issued to companies.

This declaration and any subsequent declarations will be reviewed for accuracy each year. Declarations and any revised declarations will be published on the COAG mutual recognition website and the websites of the relevant licensing authorities.

## Schedule 1: Mutual recognition matrix – Gaming Occupations

**Codes for statutes under which licences for Gaming Occupations are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gaming Machines Act 2001* or the *Casino Control Act 1992* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gambling Regulation Act 2003* or the *Casino Control Act 1991* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gaming Machine Act 1991* or the *Casino Control Act 1982* (QLD)

4\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gaming and Wagering Commission Act 1987* or the *Casino Control Act 1984* (WA)

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gaming Machines Act 1992* or the *Casino Act 1997* (SA)

6\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gaming Control Act 1993* (TAS)

7\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gaming Machine Act 2004* or the *Casino Control Act 2006* (ACT)

8\. Licences in this column issued under the *Gaming Machine Act 1995* or the *Gaming Control Act* (NT)

## Schedule 2: Mutual recognition matrix – Shotfirers

**Codes for statutes under which Shotfirer licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under *Explosives Act 2003* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Goods Act 1985* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under *Explosives Act 1999* (QLD)

4\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Goods Safety Act 2004* (WA)

5\. Licences in this column issued under *Explosives Act 1936* (SA*)*

6\. Licences in this column issued under *Explosives Act 2012* and the *Dangerous Goods Act 1998* (TAS) *(repealed 1 July 2009)*

7\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Substances Act 2004* (ACT)

8\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Goods Act 1998* (NT)

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for Scopes of Work and Restrictions for Shotfirer licences</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>S</td>
<td>Work conducted in relation to the assembly, positioning and detonation of explosives to break or dislodge rock and soil or to demolish structures, conduct seismic testing, or other special purposes. This work also includes:<br>- Check blasting areas to ensure compliance with regulations, standards and other applicable licence conditions<br>- Ensuring explosives are authorised, as per the competent authority, and within shelf life<br>- Preparation of a blast management plan<br>- Determining and maintaining the exclusion zone<br>- Ensuring site security and safety prior to/during/after blasting (e.g. falling rock hazards, underground mine roof supports and harmful fumes)<br>- Notify regulatory authority of an accident<br>- Selection of suitable explosives and method of ignition for work proposed<br>- Account for environmental impacts such as noise, overpressures, ground vibration, fly rock and air blasts<br>- Preparation of charges and insert detonators and charges into holes properly charged, stemmed and bulled and charge underground blasts<br>- Use of boosters<br>- Decide quantity of explosives required<br>- Implement warning procedures<br>- Connect and test or inspect the blasting circuit<br>- Fire charges<br>- Inspect area to make sure all explosives have detonated<br>- Treatment of misfired explosives<br>- Disposal of unused and/or out of date explosives</td>
</tr>

<tr>
<td>Q/OC</td>
<td>Quarry/Open Cut Mining</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>R</td>
<td>Road Works</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Se</td>
<td>Seismic Work excludes OB</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SFX</td>
<td>Special Effects Work - Film and TV (using blasting explosives, propellants, ammunition - not before a proximate audience)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>T</td>
<td>Tunnel Work</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>TR</td>
<td>Trench work</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>UC</td>
<td>Underground Coal Mining</td>
</tr>
</table>

**Instructions for reading the exclusions and restrictions on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘restricted to’ or ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions on the functions and situation, sites and application methods within the scope of the licence. Note scope of work code S is common for every shotfirer licence.

## Schedule 3: Mutual recognition matrix – Pyrotechnicians

**Codes for statutes under which Pyrotechnicians licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Substances Act 2004* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Goods Act 1985* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under *Explosives Act 1936* (QLD)

4\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Goods Safety Act 2004* (WA)

5\. Licences in this column issued under *Explosives Act 1936* (SA)

6\. Licences in this column issued under *Explosives Act 2012 (*TAS)

7\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Substances Act 2004* (ACT)

8\. Licences in this column issued under *Dangerous Goods Act 1998* (NT)

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for scopes of work and restrictions for Pyrotechnicians licences</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>P</td>
<td>Work conducted in relation to the assembly, positioning and firing of ground, aerial fireworks and theatrical fireworks (aka close proximity fireworks), and:<br>- Check display areas to make sure that safety regulations are met<br>- The on-site risk assessment and management of safety, at the display, as outlined in the safety management plan or as otherwise authorised by the Fireworks Contractor.<br>- Ensure site security and safety, including crowd management and creation of a prohibited area, prior to /during and after (e.g. unexploded fireworks, support stakes, empty casings) the display<br>- Liaise with key stakeholders – local council, police and any other relevant bodies and land owner<br>- Decide quantity of fireworks required<br>Assemble fuses, firing circuits and charges; test or inspect the electronic firing device where applicable and fire charges</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>P1</td>
<td>- Conduct risk assessment including assessment of environmental conditions (including wind and fire restrictions) and implement safe work procedures in accordance with risk assessment<br>- Design fireworks display suitable for the display site</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>P2</td>
<td>Testing of shop goods fireworks<br>- Check testing ground to make sure that safety regulations are met<br>- Check testing environment where shop goods fireworks will be dismantled<br>- Check ignition method and fuse burning time<br>- Check pyrotechnic content and composition<br>- Check construction type and integrity<br>- Check performance of the firework<br>- Check debris characteristics<br>- Testing of shop goods fireworks<br>- Check testing ground to make sure that safety regulations are met</td>
</tr>
</table>

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Type of pyrotechnics/fireworks</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A≤300</td>
<td>Aerial shells up to and including 300mm</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>AU</td>
<td>Aerial shells unlimited size</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>AS≤75</td>
<td>Aerial salutes up to and including 75mm</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>CF/S</td>
<td>Chinese Firecrackers/Chinese String Fireworks</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>G</td>
<td>Ground display fireworks</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>TF</td>
<td>Theatrical fireworks or special effects fireworks used in close proximity (Articles)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MIX</td>
<td>The making and use of special effects and theatrical fireworks in situ (not TF)</td>
</tr>
</table>

**Instructions for reading the exclusions and restrictions on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘restricted to’ or ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions on the functions and situation, sites and application methods within the scope of the licence. Note the scope of work Codes P and P1 are common for every pyrotechnicians licence.

## Schedule 4: Mutual recognition matrix – Pest and Weed Controllers

**Codes for statutes under which licences for Pest and Weed Controllers are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the Occupational Health and Safety Act 2000 or Pesticides Act 1999 (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Public Health and Wellbeing Act 2008* or *Agricultural and Veterinary Chemicals (Control of Use) Act 1992* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Pest Management Act 2001* or *Agricultural Chemicals Distribution Control Act 1966* (QLD)

4\. Licences in this column issued under the Health Act 1911 or Aerial Spraying Control Act 1966 (WA)

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Controlled Substances Act 1984* (SA)

6\. Licences in this column issued under the *Agricultural and Veterinary Chemicals (Control of Use) Act 1995* (TAS)

7\. Licences in this column issued under the *Environment Protection Act 1997* (ACT)

8\. Licences in this column issued under the *Medicines, Poisons and Therapeutics Goods Act 2012* or *Agricultural and Veterinary Chemicals (Control of Use) Act 2004* (NT)

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for licence levels</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>(S)</td>
<td>A person must be supervised by a licensed/registered operator to be allowed to exercise the functions of the licensed operator.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>(U)</td>
<td>Person is able to undertake controlled activities on their own account without having to be employed and supervised by another licensed/registered operator.</td>
</tr>
</table>

**Agreed Scopes of Work for Pest and Weed Controllers**

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for functions</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>AP</td>
<td>The control, destruction or repelling of arthropod pests (e.g. European Wasps) but excluding timber pests and plant arthropod pests.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>LV</td>
<td>The control, destruction or repelling of large vertebrate pests (eg. foxes, rabbits).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>PP</td>
<td>The control, destruction or repelling of plant arthropod pests and diseases.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SV</td>
<td>The control, destruction or repelling of small vertebrate pests (eg. birds, rodents).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>TP</td>
<td>The control, destruction or repelling of timber pests (eg. termites).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>WD</td>
<td>The control, destruction or repelling of plants that are weeds.</td>
</tr>
</table>

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for situations</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>AG</td>
<td>The application of a pesticide in, on or immediately around an agricultural building or other structure dealing with the storage of agricultural products. May include: agricultural storage areas (eg. On - and off - farm and commercial grain silos, other agricultural storage arrangements, post harvest treatments); a vessel (ship or boat), vehicle (includes a motor vehicle, tractor, train) or aircraft, that holds either agricultural produce or is used in agricultural activities.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>CR</td>
<td>The application of pesticide for an agricultural crop purposes (including viticulture, horticultural and pasture situations).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>FO</td>
<td>The application of a pesticide in forestry situations.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>NA</td>
<td>The application of a pesticide in or on a non-agricultural building (including domestic or commercial) or other structures. May include: a storage area (warehouse, shed etc); a vessel (ship or boat) or a vehicle (includes a motor vehicle, train) or aircraft, and the land immediately around domestic residences and commercial buildings.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>NC</td>
<td>The application of a pesticide in non-crop situations. May include: recreational turf situations on land (including public parks, the land around clubhouses and amenities), industrial sites (not including built structures); timber yards; rights of way; irrigation channels; wastelands; bushlands; cemeteries etc.</td>
</tr>
</table>

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for application methods</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>AA</td>
<td>The spraying, spreading or dispersing of any pesticide from an aircraft in flight. Includes baits.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>FG</td>
<td>Fumigation means any treatment carried out with a fumigant. A fumigant is a substance that, when in a gaseous or vaporous state, is used to control or destroy pests.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>GM</td>
<td>The ground application of pesticides by a machine or apparatus powered other than by hand - excluding application by an aircraft in flight or by fumigation.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>HA</td>
<td>The ground application of pesticides by a hand held appliance (excluding fumigation). A hand held appliance includes a small portable spray that can be carried and operated manually.</td>
</tr>
</table>

**Instructions for reading the restrictions on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘restricted to’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions on the functions and situation, sites and application methods within the scope of the licence.

## Schedule 5: Mutual recognition matrix – Asbestos Removal

**Codes for statutes under which Asbestos Removal licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Occupational Health and Safety Act 2000* (NSW)\
Code of Practice for the Safe Removal of Asbestos (NOHSC:2002 (1998)\
Worksafe Australia Code of Practice for the Safe Removal of Asbestos

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Occupational Health and Safety Regulations 2007* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Workplace Health and Safety Regulation 1997* (QLD)\
Code of Practice for the Management and Control of Asbestos in Workplaces [NOHSC:2018 (2005)]\
Code of Practice for the Safe Removal of Asbestos (NOHSC:2002 (1998)

4\. Licences in this column issued under the *Occupational Safety and Health Regulations 1996* (WA)

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Occupational Health, Safety and Welfare Act 1986* (SA)\
Code of Practice for the Safe Removal of Asbestos 2nd edition (NOHSC:2002 (2005)

6\. Licences in this column issued under the *Workplace Health and Safety Act 1995* (TAS)\
Code of Practice for the Safe Removal of Asbestos (NOHSC:2002 (1998)

7\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 2004* (ACT), the *Construction Occupations (Licensing) Act 2004* and *Dangerous Substances Act 2004*\
Code of Practice for the Safe Removal of Asbestos (NOHSC:2002 (2005)

8\. Licences in this column issued under the *Workplace Health and Safety Act 2007* (NT)\
Code of Practice for the Safe Removal of Asbestos 2nd Edition(NOHSC:2002 (2005)

**Agreed definition for Asbestos**

Friable asbestos is asbestos containing material which when dry can be crumbled, pulverised or reduced to powder by hand pressure. Examples of friable asbestos are sprayed asbestos coating or insulation, asbestos lagging, loose asbestos and in its raw form. This may include asbestos-containing material which ends up in this state because of weathering, physical damage or deterioration under its conditions of use.

Non-friable or bonded asbestos includes asbestos in a form where the asbestos fibre is held within another material but does not include friable asbestos. An example of non-friable asbestos is where asbestos is bonded within cement based board.

**Agreed codes for scopes of work for Asbestos**

| A1 | Removal and preparation for disposal of all forms of asbestos. |
|---|---|
| A2 | Removal and preparation for disposal of more than 10m² or more of bonded (non-friable) asbestos. |

**Instructions for reading the exclusions and limitations on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘restricted to’ or ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions on the functions and situation, sites and application methods within the scope of the licence.

## Schedule 6: Mutual recognition matrix - Demolition

**Codes for statutes under which Demolition licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Occupational Health and Safety Act 2000* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 1993* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Workplace Health and Safety Act 1995* (QLD)

4\. Licences in this column issued under the *Occupational Safety and Health Regulations 1996 (WA)*

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 2000* (TAS)

7\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 2004* and *Construction Occupations (Licensing) Act 2004* (ACT)

8\. Not licenced in the NT

**Agreed Scope of Work for Demolition**

Demolition work means the partial or complete dismantling and removal, by manual or mechanical means, of a building or structure, or part thereof.\
Work includes structural demolition with stripping of internal fittings, fixtures, services and non-load bearing elements.

**Agreed Scopes of Work for Earthworks/Excavations**

Excavating earth, ore and rock.

Break up of pavement, road, rock and obstructions.

Moving and loading of earth, rock and debris.

Levelling, smoothing and compacting surfaces in construction and other projects.

## Schedule 8: Mutual recognition matrix - Fencing

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989 (*NSW)

Fencing means the construction, dismantling, maintenance and repair of fences and gates including metal and timber gates (either manual or automatic) within or on property boundary lines.

## Schedule 10: Mutual recognition matrix - Foundation Work

**Codes for statutes under which Foundation Work licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 1993* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in the ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for Scopes of Work for Foundation Work</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>FW1</td>
<td>Foundation work (which includes underpinning, piering, piling and anchoring) means the construction of a new support structure under or adjoining a wall, column or footings of a building without removing the superstructure and includes concreting, shoring and dewatering the site.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>FW2</td>
<td>Foundation work may include new foundation work for proposed buildings and structures.</td>
</tr>
</table>

**Instructions for reading the exclusions and limitations on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions on the functions and situation, sites and application methods within the scope of the licence.

## Schedule 12: Mutual recognition matrix - Glazing - Windows and Doors

**Codes for statutes under which Glazing - Windows and Doors licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Not licenced in VIC

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995 (*SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

**Agreed Scope of Work for Glazing - Windows and Doors**

Glazing means the work of installing glass, acrylic or other like materials in prepared openings or glazed assemblies, and includes frames, glass, screens (but not security screens), windows, door panels, or partitions.

## Schedule 13: Mutual recognition matrix - Kitchens, Bathrooms and Laundries

**Codes for statutes under which Kitchens, Bathrooms and Laundries licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed Scope of Work for Kitchens, Bathrooms and Laundries**

Kitchen, bathroom and laundry work means the installation, refurbishment, restoration and on-site repairs of or to a kitchen, bathroom or laundry of a residential building or small commercial kitchen (commercial work not regulated in NSW). This work must be contained within the existing building envelope and does not include structural alteration.

## Schedule 14: Mutual recognition matrix - Metal Fabrication

**Codes for statutes under which licences Metal Fabrication are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Not licenced in VIC

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for Scopes of Work for Metal Fabrication</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MF1</td>
<td>The construction of structural steel work.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MF2</td>
<td>The construction and installation of ornamental metalwork.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MF3</td>
<td>The construction and installation of metal handrails.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MF4</td>
<td>The construction and installation of security grilles, balustrades and the like.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MF5</td>
<td>The preparation, fabrication and erection of structural and non-structural metal components</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MF6</td>
<td>The construction and installation of metal fencing.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>MF7</td>
<td>The construction of light steel framing.</td>
</tr>
</table>

**Instructions for reading the exclusions on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions within the scope of the licence.

## Schedule 15: Mutual recognition matrix - Painting and Decorating

**Codes for statutes under which Painting and Decorating licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Not licenced in VIC

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Licences in this column issued under the *Painters’ Registration Act 1961* (WA)

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed definition of Painting and Decorating**

Painting means the application of clear, resin based or pigmented product in liquid form which, when applied to a surface by brush, roller or spray gun, forms a dry, adherent film.

Decorating means the work of two dimensional painted or applied decorations such as murals, frescos, wallpaper etc.

**Codes for Scopes of Work for Painting and Decorating**

| D1 | Apply two dimensional painted or applied decorations such as murals, frescos, wallpaper. |
|---|---|
| P1 | Apply textured coatings by brush, roller or spray gun. |
| P2 | Minor repair (but not renewal of replacement) of surfaces prior to painting. |
| P3 | Apply paint or other substance for protective, decorative or technical purposes, including colour matching. |
| P4 | Apply texture coatings. |
| P5 | Prepare surfaces for application of paint or other protective, decorative or technical materials. |

**Instructions for reading the exclusions and limitations on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘restricted to’ or ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions within the scope of the licence.

## Schedule 16: Mutual recognition matrix - Pools and Spas

**Codes for statutes under which Pools and Spas licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 1993* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Licences in this column issued under the *Construction Occupations (Licensing) Act 2004* (ACT)

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed Definition for Swimming Pool and Spa Pool Work**

Swimming Pool means work involved in the construction, renovation, alteration, repair, extension, maintenance, decorative or protective treatment of a swimming pool. Spa pool means work involved in the construction, renovation, alteration, repair, extension, maintenance, decorative or protective treatment of a spa pool.

**Codes for Scopes of Work for Pools**\
Licences shown in the table below may include some or all of these scopes of work

| SP1 | Carry out earthworks and drainage for swimming pool installation and construction. |
|---|---|
| SP2 | Place and fix reinforcement for concrete for swimming pool installation construction |
| SP3 | Install formwork to define a swimming pool |
| SP4 | Place and finish concrete or other materials to provide a shape or form for a swimming pool,. |
| SP5 | Install prefabricated swimming pools including packing, pool units filling and levelling of prefabricated |
| SP6 | Carry out ancillary pipework including general filtration, sanitation, water chemistry, solar heating and basic hydraulics within pool system. |
| SP7 | Carry out landscaping works associated with the construction of a swimming pool including pool fencing |
| SP8 | Carry out ceramic tiling, painting, paving, and surfacing (rendering, plastering or application of surface coatings) associated with the construction of a swimming pool. |
| SP9 | Prepare plans and specifications if the plans and specifications are for the licensee’s personal use or for use in building work performed by the licensee personally |

**Codes for Scopes of Work for Spas**\
Licences shown in the table above may include some or all of these scopes of work

| S1 | Carry out earthworks and drainage for spa installation and construction. |
|---|---|
| S2 | Place and fix reinforcement for concrete for spa installation construction |
| S3 | Install formwork to define a spa. |
| S4 | Place and finish concrete or other materials to provide a shape or form for a spa, . |
| S5 | Install prefabricated spa pools. including packing, filling and levelling of prefabricated spa units |
| S6 | Carry out ancillary pipework including general filtration, sanitation, water chemistry, solar heating and basic hydraulics within pool system |
| S7 | Carry out landscaping works associated with the construction of a spa including pool fencing. |
| S8 | Carry out ceramic tiling, painting, paving, and plastering associated with the construction of a spa pool. |

**Instructions for reading the exclusions and limitations on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘restricted to’ or ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions on the functions and situation, sites and application methods within the scope of the licence.

**Codes for statutes under which licences for Retaining Walls are issued**

1\. Not licenced in NSW

4\. Not licenced in WA

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed Scopes of Work for Retaining Walls.**

Construct reinforced/composite concrete walls.\
Construct retaining walls using brick and block work and other construction materials including sleepers.\
Construct retaining walls using pre-cast and interlocking proprietary systems.

## Schedule 18: Mutual recognition matrix - Roof Tiling

**Codes for statutes under which Roof Tiling licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 1993* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed Scope of Work for Roof Tiling.**

Roof tiling means the fixing of roof tiles or slate to the exterior surface of roof and fascia structures. It does not include the structural alteration of roof members/components other than tile battens.

## Schedule 19: Mutual recognition matrix - Shopfitting

**Codes for statutes under which licences for Shop-fitting are issued**

1\. Not licenced in NSW

2\. Not licenced in VIC

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Licences in this column issued under the *Construction Occupations (Licensing) Act 2004* (ACT)

8\. Not licenced in NT

<table>
<tr>
<td colspan="2"><b>Codes for Scopes of Work for Shop-fitting</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF1</td>
<td>The preparation of plans and specifications if the plans and specifications are for the licensees’ personal use or for use in building work to be performed by the licensee personally.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF2</td>
<td>Set out, fabricate and assemble cabinets, showcases, wall units, counters and work stations.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF3</td>
<td>Set out, fabricate and assemble shopfronts, commercial entries, bulkheads and component fittings.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF4</td>
<td>Install sub-floor framing, including bearers, joints and ladder frames.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF5</td>
<td>Construct and erect wall and ceiling framing including install internal linings, assemble partition frames and install curtain walling.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF6</td>
<td>Manufacture, assemble and fit components for door and window frames, doors and sashes.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF7</td>
<td>Manufacture, assemble and install stairs.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF8</td>
<td>Manufacture, assemble and install joinery unit components.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF9</td>
<td>Apply paint and other finishes, including prepare surfaces for painting.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF10</td>
<td>Cut and install glass.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF11</td>
<td>Carry out aluminium work including fabricate, assemble or construct framework and manufacture aluminium grills and louvres.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF12</td>
<td>Incidental work of another licence class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF13</td>
<td>Does not include Type A or Type B construction.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF14</td>
<td>Does not include building work to the superstructure of the building.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>SF15</td>
<td>Does not include personally carrying out any building work for which a fire protection licence is required or an occupational licence is required.</td>
</tr>
</table>

**Instructions for reading the exclusions on licences**

## Schedule 21: Mutual recognition matrix - Solid (wet) Plastering and Dry Plastering

**Codes for statutes under which Solid (wet) Plastering and Dry Plastering licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 1993* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed Scope of Work for Solid (wet) Plastering and Dry Plastering**

Dry plastering or drywall plastering means the installation of rigid building sheeting/boards made of a core of gypsum plaster set between and bonded to outer covers of cardboard. It does not include completed work that provides structural support or any external cladding works which are considered to be aligned with carpentry or joinery trades.

Solid (wet) plastering means the application of a mixture of lime, cement or gypsum plaster and/or sand and water (or another material to achieve a similar result) for the covering of internal or external walls and ceilings.

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Codes for Scopes of Work for Stonemasonry**\
Licences shown in the table below may include some or all of these scopes of work.

| SM 1 | Setting stone in mortar and fixing including granite, stone walling, stone veneers, staircases, decorative work, pointing, cleaning, flashing etc |
|---|---|
| SM 2 | Work with stone, including for example finishing, laying, machining, renovating and shaping of stone |
| SM 3 | Constructing with stone, including for example, arches, fireplaces, stairs and walls |
| SM 4 | Carry out minor non-structural concrete work ancillary to stonemasonry |
| SM 5 | Laying segmental or unit paving for stonemasonry |
| SM 6 | Setting out and anchoring facades |
| SM 7 | Applying and installing waterproofing and damp-proofing for stonemasonry |

**Instructions for reading the exclusions on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions within the scope of the licence.

## Schedule 23: Mutual recognition matrix - Wall and Floor Tiling

**Codes for statutes under which Wall and Floor Tiling licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Not licenced in VIC

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed Scope of Work for Wall and Floor Tiling**

Wall and Floor Tiling means the affixing of tiles for functional or decorative use on internal or external surfaces of buildings.

**Codes for Scopes of Work for Wall and Floor Tiling**\
Licences shown in the table below may include some or all of these scopes of work.

| WF1 | Cutting and fixing tiles, including ceramic, glass, marble, slate, stone and terracotta tiles, to fireplaces, floors, hearths, spas, swimming pools and walls |
|---|---|
| WF2 | Constructing terrazzo floors, steps, risers and stringers |
| WF3 | The application of waterproofing for wall and floor tiling. |
| WF4 | Fixing tiles made from cork, linoleum, rubber or thermoplastics and mosaics |
| WF5 | Preparation and minor repair (but not renewal or replacement) and insertion of expansion joints to surfaces prior to tiling |

**Instructions for reading the exclusions and limitations on licences**

Where a licence in the second jurisdiction is marked ‘restricted to’ or ‘excluding’, the coding immediately below the licence name refers to specific conditions within the scope of the licence.

## Schedule 24: Mutual recognition matrix - Waterproofing

**Codes for statutes under which Waterproofing licences are issued**

1\. Licences in this column issued under the *Home Building Act 1989* (NSW)

2\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Act 1993* (VIC)

3\. Licences in this column issued under the *Queensland Building Services Authority Act 1991* (QLD)

4\. Not licenced in WA

5\. Licences in this column issued under the *Building Work Contractors Act 1995* (SA)

6\. Not licenced in TAS

7\. Not licenced in ACT

8\. Not licenced in NT

**Agreed definition for Waterproofing**

Waterproofing means work involving any protective treatment of a dwelling, building or structure designed to prevent the penetration of water or moisture into a dwelling, building or structure or the protective treatment of wet areas in a dwelling designed to prevent the unwanted escape of water from those areas by using solid membranes or membranes applied by brush, roller or any other method.

**Codes for Scopes of Work for Waterproofing**\
Licences shown in the table below may include some or all of these scopes of work.

| W1 | The use of membranes to the interior, exterior, below ground and remedial areas of a dwelling, building or structure. |
|---|---|
| WP | Applying, installing and repairing waterproofing including surface preparation and applying or installing material or systems for preventing moisture penetration of a building, dwelling or structure.. |

## Endnotes

### Endnote 1—About the endnotes

The endnotes provide details of the history of this legislation and its provisions. The following endnotes are included in each compilation:

Endnote 1—About the endnotes

Endnote 2—Abbreviation key

Endnote 3—Legislation history

Endnote 4—Amendment history

Endnote 5—Uncommenced amendments

Endnote 6—Modifications

Endnote 7—Misdescribed amendments

Endnote 8—Miscellaneous

If there is no information under a particular endnote, the word “none” will appear in square brackets after the endnote heading.

**Abbreviation key—Endnote 2**

The abbreviation key in this endnote sets out abbreviations that may be used in the endnotes.

**Legislation history and amendment history—Endnotes 3 and 4**

Amending laws are annotated in the legislation history and amendment history.

The legislation history in endnote 3 provides information about each law that has amended the compiled law. The information includes commencement information for amending laws and details of application, saving or transitional provisions that are not included in this compilation.

The amendment history in endnote 4 provides information about amendments at the provision level. It also includes information about any provisions that have expired or otherwise ceased to have effect in accordance with a provision of the compiled law.

**Uncommenced amendments—Endnote 5**

The effect of uncommenced amendments is not reflected in the text of the compiled law, but the text of the amendments is included in endnote 5.

**Modifications—Endnote 6**

If the compiled law is affected by a modification that is in force, details of the modification are included in endnote 6.

**Misdescribed amendments—Endnote 7**

An amendment is a misdescribed amendment if the effect of the amendment cannot be incorporated into the text of the compilation. Any misdescribed amendment is included in endnote 7.

**Miscellaneous—Endnote 8**

Endnote 8 includes any additional information that may be helpful for a reader of the compilation.
